### Recreational use of drones

In general, the use of drones for recreational purposes is allowed in Vatnajökull National Park, but regional restrictions apply in some areas.

### How to pay for camping & regional fees

For the campsite in Ásbyrgi and regional fees for Skaftafell and Jökulsárlón.

### Permits

For research, film making, commercials and events.

## Plan your visit

Vatnajökull National Park offers wide a range of traditional outdoor recreational activities for visitor enjoyment including hiking, driving, camping and bird watching. We recommend exporing the website and then starting at our visitor centres to get updates on services, roads and weather.

Things to do

![](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2Fab32b869-6ef5-4fdf-b377-e485af56a0ef_esjufjoll-6043.jpg%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfaces%252Cedges%26fit%3Dcrop%26w%3D800%26h%3D1000.150489089541&w=3840&q=80)

## Travel & safety advice

Travelling in Iceland requires preparation because the weather is ever-changing and circumstances can vary greatly. Vatnajökull National Park mostly encompasses mountainous territory and land near to mountains, including parts of the highlands of Iceland where summers are short and weather conditions can change suddenly.

Travel advice

## Visitor Centres & destinations in Vatnajökull National Park

Vatnajökull National Park is the largest of national parks in Iceland and a Unesco World Heritage site. It holds some of the most popular destinations in Iceland. Travelers must be prepared for ever changing weather and conditions.

## The National Park of fire & ice *- Learn & explore*

Vatnajökull National Park is unique on a global level due to its geological history, which has been largely written by the protracted conflict between fire and ice.

![](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2Fa0c96795-542b-41e0-94b4-6c341d5fa8ae_Sn%25C3%25A6fell_%25C3%259ArDreka_19_SEV.JPG%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfaces%252Cedges%26fit%3Dcrop%26w%3D800%26h%3D533.5289801907556&w=3840&q=80)

## Unesco World Heritage

Vatnajökull National Park was included on the UNESCO World Heritage List on 5 July 2019 on the basis of its unique natural characteristics.

Learn about Unesco world heritage

![](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2Fdda57684-1618-426c-9463-2034b59e4c22_3_10b_skridjokull_sg_HJ.jpg%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfaces%252Cedges%26fit%3Dcrop%26w%3D800%26h%3D531.25&w=3840&q=80)

## Melting glaciers

Information about climate change and the associated consequences for glaciers in Iceland and elsewhere.

Learn about melting glaciers

![](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2F6514a4a9-cb51-44e5-8848-4875c423f4e5_rj%25C3%25BApa%2BSKF_SRR%2B19.JPG%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfaces%252Cedges%26fit%3Dcrop%26w%3D660%26h%3D438.3582089552239&w=3840&q=80)

## Nature & history

The landscape of the National Park is shaped by the synergy between volcanoes and glaciers. Vatnajökull National Park and the neighbouring areas have many and varied cultural remains and there are tales of human habitation and farming in the area over the centuries.

Learn about the nature & history

![](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2F1ffb2ce2-9a56-4ca3-90a0-66973ff0a86c_Askur_Askja_19_SEV.JPG%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfaces%252Cedges%26fit%3Dcrop%26w%3D800%26h%3D533.5289801907556&w=3840&q=80)

## Management and Conservation Plan

The Management and Conservation Plan is the principal tool for managing the National Park. The current version (3rd edition) of the Management and Conservation Plan entered into effect on 5 July 2022.

Learn more

![](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2Ff1483d70-7082-45af-be0b-6e55539ed487_frontpage-hero.jpg%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfaces%252Cedges%26fit%3Dcrop%26w%3D1200%26h%3D1200&w=3840&q=80)

# [2] https://www.vatnajokulsthjodgardur.is/en/news/natturuverndarstofnun-tekin-til-starfa

# The Nature Conservation Agency of Iceland is established

On January 1st 2025, the Nature Conservation Agency of Iceland was established.

2. January 2025

![](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2FZ3ZbZJbqstJ98-BC_N%25C3%25A1tt%25C3%25BArverndarstofnunmynd.png%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfaces%252Cedges%26fit%3Dcrop%26w%3D1640%26h%3D820&w=3840&q=80)

The Nature Conservation Agency of Iceland handles projects related to nature conservation, biodiversity and hunting management within the Environmental Agency and the operations of Vatnajökull National Park. The agency is responsible for administration, supervision, and other tasks in the fields of nature conservation, sustainable development, protected areas, protection of wild birds and mammals, and fisheries management. Additionally, the agency coordinates the regional management and protection planning and monitoring in the above-mentioned divisions.

The agency's website is under development, but it can be found here www.nattura.is. The website of the Environmental Agency will therefore be accessible for the time being, and changes can be expected on the website of Vatnajökull National Park, which will remain accessible, as will the website of Snæfellsjökull National Park.

### Notkun dróna í afþreyingarskyni

Almennt er notkun flygilda (dróna) í afþreyingarskyni heimil í Vatnajökulsþjóðgarði en á nokkrum svæðum gilda þó svæðisbundnar takmarkanir.

### Greiða fyrir tjaldsvæði og svæðisgjöld

Fyrir tjaldsvæði í Ásbyrgi og svæðisgjöld (innheimt á bílastæðum) í Skaftafelli og við Jökulsárlón.

## Skipuleggðu ferðalagið

Vatnajökulsþjóðgarður býður upp á fjölbreytta möguleika til útvistar í stórbrotinni náttúru. Skoðaðu gestastofur, áfangastaði og þjónustu eins og tjaldsvæði og skálagistingu.

Sjá hvað er hægt að gera

![](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2Fab32b869-6ef5-4fdf-b377-e485af56a0ef_esjufjoll-6043.jpg%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfaces%252Cedges%26fit%3Dcrop%26w%3D800%26h%3D1000.150489089541&w=3840&q=80)

## Góð ferðaráð fyrir þig og náttúruna

Við viljum hjálpa þér og náttúrunni að njóta ferðalagsins. Undirbúningur er lykillinn að góðu ferðalagi, að þekkja til aðstæðna á svæðinu og kynna sér færð og veður.

Ferðaráð

## Áfangastaðir í Vatnajökuls­þjóðgarði

Vatnajökulsþjóðgarður hefur að geyma marga þekktustu áfangastaði landsins en einnig fáfarnari perlur. Stór hluti þjóðgarðsins er á hálendi og því þarf að skipuleggja ferðalög innan þjóðgarðins vel.

## Þjóðgarður elds og ísa *- fræðsla og uppgötvun*

Vatnajökulsþjóðgarður er einstakur á heimsvísu vegna jarðsögu sinnar sem einkum er skrifuð af átökum elds og íss. Þjóðgarðurinn skartar eldfjöllum, jöklum en einnig átökum flekaskila, möttulstróks og hveljökuls. Samspils þessara fyrirbæra og annarra landmótunarafla hefur skapað fjölbreyttari náttúru en finna má á nokkru afmörkuðu svæði í heiminum og er ástæða þess að þjóðgarðurinn er á heimsminjaskrá UNESCO.

![](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2Fa0c96795-542b-41e0-94b4-6c341d5fa8ae_Sn%25C3%25A6fell_%25C3%259ArDreka_19_SEV.JPG%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfaces%252Cedges%26fit%3Dcrop%26w%3D800%26h%3D533.5289801907556&w=3840&q=80)

## Hvað er heimsminjaskrá?

Heimsminjar eiga oft stóran sess í sögu og sjálfsmynd þjóða. Til að komast á heimsminjaskrá UNESCO þarf staður eða fyrirbæri að hafa það sem kallað er einstakt gildi á heimsvísu. Vatnajökulsþjóðgarður var samþykktur á heimsminjaskránna hinn 5. júlí 2019 á grundvelli einstakrar náttúru.

Meira um heimsminjar

![](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2Fdda57684-1618-426c-9463-2034b59e4c22_3_10b_skridjokull_sg_HJ.jpg%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfaces%252Cedges%26fit%3Dcrop%26w%3D800%26h%3D531.25&w=3840&q=80)

## Hörfandi jöklar

Upplýsingar um loftslagsbreytingar og áhrif þeirra á jökla á Íslandi sem og annars staðar á jörðinni. Jöklar á Íslandi hafa hopað hratt í rúma tvo áratugi og er rýrnun þeirra einhver helsta afleiðing hlýnandi loftslags hérlendis og skýr vitnisburður um hlýnunina.

Fræðsla um hörfandi jökla

![](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2F6514a4a9-cb51-44e5-8848-4875c423f4e5_rj%25C3%25BApa%2BSKF_SRR%2B19.JPG%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfaces%252Cedges%26fit%3Dcrop%26w%3D660%26h%3D438.3582089552239&w=3840&q=80)

![](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2Fc0d31fbf-5095-4cf0-ab22-59755ed10672_Lambagras_Stefani%25CC%2581aR.JPG%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfaces%252Cedges%26fit%3Dcrop%26w%3D533%26h%3D354.0074626865672&w=3840&q=80)

## Náttúra & saga

Náttúra Vatnajökulsþjóðgarðs er kvik og síbreytileg. Eldvirknin byggir landið upp en roföflin, jöklar, vatn og vindar, brjóta það niður. Ungt landið tekur stöðugum breytingum. Jöklar hörfa, jökulár hlaupa fram og breyta um farveg og jökullón verða til, stækka eða tæmast. Gróður nemur land og vex hann og dafnar lengur en getur þó á endanum þurft að láta undan roföflum náttúrunnar. Dýralíf mótast af gróðurfari hvers tíma og mannlífið mótast af kraftmiklum og kvikum náttúruöflunum.

Fræðsla um náttúru & sögu

## Þjóðgarðurinn okkar *- leiðandi í sjálfbærni*

![](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2Fe1feae15-49dc-48be-9b5c-551ad03b80d7_F%25C3%25B6grufj%25C3%25B6ll_SB.jpg%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfaces%252Cedges%26fit%3Dcrop%26w%3D800%26h%3D530&w=3840&q=80)

## Stefna Vatnajökulsþjóðgarðs

Mikið uppbyggingastarf hefur átt sér stað í Vatnajökulsþjóðgarði á undanförnum árum. Mikilvægt er að þróa starfsemina áfram með markvissum hætti. Ákveðið var undir lok árs 2020 að skerpa kúrsinn, móta skýra stefnu og varða leiðina til næstu ára. Meginmarkmið í stefnunni eru verndun, upplifun, sköpun og stjórnun.

Skoða stefnu þjóðgarðsins

![](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2F1ffb2ce2-9a56-4ca3-90a0-66973ff0a86c_Askur_Askja_19_SEV.JPG%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfaces%252Cedges%26fit%3Dcrop%26w%3D800%26h%3D533.5289801907556&w=3840&q=80)

## Stjórnunar - og verndaráætlun

Stjórnunar- og verndaráætlun er aðalstjórntæki þjóðgarðsins. Áætlunin er byggð á tillögum svæðisráða og stjórnar og mótuð í samráði við hagsmunaaðila. Tilgangur hennar er að feta slóð jafnvægis milli verndunar og landnýtingar með grunnstefið fólgið í markmiðum þjóðgarðsins um verndun, aðgengi, fræðslu og byggðaþróun.

Skoða Stjórnunar- og verndaráætlun

Due to staff meeting of the Nature Conservation Agency of Iceland, the opening hours of the visitor centres of Vatnajökull National Park will be reduced from November 13th-15th. We apologize for any inconvenience this may cause.

11. November 2025

Unchanged opening hours (10 AM - 5 PM)

# [6] https://www.vatnajokulsthjodgardur.is/en/news/landslide-by-dettifoss

# Landslide by Dettifoss

A large landslide has fallen from the cliffs of Jökulsárgljúfur, close to the viewing platform just northwest of Dettifoss. Newest update on the condition are on Jökulsárgljúfur facebook page: https://www.facebook.com/Jokulsargljufur

5. June 2025

![](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2FaEG9Q7h8WN-LVrNK_493855293_1140744501429574_6444035726798242874_n.jpg%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfaces%252Cedges%26fit%3Dcrop%26w%3D1640%26h%3D820&w=3840&q=80)

As reported before the weekend, a landslide occurred right by the viewing platform. During a closer inspection the crack still appears to be very unstable. Cracks that were not known before were also discovered in the area. For safety reasons, it was decided to close the northernmost part of the viewing platform as well as the path down to the waterfall. It is not considered safe to open those spots again until experts have assessed the situation.

It is still possible to go and see Dettifoss, both from the southern part of the viewing platform and from the viewpoint above the waterfall.

# [8] https://www.vatnajokulsthjodgardur.is/en/news/christmas-in-vatnajokull-national-park

# Christmas in Vatnajökull National Park

The staff of Vatnajökull National Park wishes you a joyful holiday season and prosperity in the coming year. We draw your attention to the changed opening hours of the visitor centers during the holidays.

20. December 2024

![](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2FZ2QaLZbqstJ98ral_J%25C3%25B3lVJ%25C3%259E-3-.png%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfaces%252Cedges%26fit%3Dcrop%26w%3D1640%26h%3D820&w=3840&q=80)

During the darkest winter months, we kindly remind our visitors to travel with caution and carefully consider weather and conditions at all times. At the most popular areas of the national park, services are available year-round in the form of education and information at visitor centres. However, please note the adjusted opening hours during the holidays:

**Gígur Visitor Centre**

* December 24: 10–14
* December 25-27: Closed
* December 28–31: 10–14 (regular hours)
* January 1: Closed

**Gljúfrastofa Visitor Centre**

* Closed from December 20 to January 2

**Skaftafellsstofa Visitor Centre**

* December 24: 10–14
* December 25: 12–16
* December 26–30: 10–17 (regular hours)
* December 31: 10–16
* January 1: 12–16

**Skaftárstofa Visitor Centre**

* December 24–26: 09–13
* December 27–30: 09–17 (regular hours)
* December 31 & January 1: 09–13

**Snæfellstofa Visitor Centre**

* Closed

We encourage all visitors to plan ahead and stay safe during their travels.

# [10] https://www.vatnajokulsthjodgardur.is/en/thenationalpark/drone-rules

# Rules for the use of drones for recreational purposes

In general, the use of drones for recreational purposes is allowed in Vatnajökull National Park, but regional restrictions apply in some areas. The general rules of the Icelandic Transport Authority must be followed, but the rules of Vatnajökull National Park (general and regional) below also apply. Special permits for recreational drone usage are not issued, but users are asked to follow the guidelines on this webpage instead.

![](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2F8c886e73-f253-471c-81b1-889a452dbddb_Fagrafell_ALA.jpg%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfocalpoint%26fit%3Dcrop%26fp-x%3D0.37%26fp-y%3D0.50%26w%3D1640%26h%3D820&w=3840&q=80)

## **General authorization for the use of drones**

In addition to the Icelandic Transport Authority’s general rules on the use of drones, the following rules apply within Vatnajökull National Park (general and regional).

The rules are intended to contribute to the following goals of Vatnajökull National Park:

- Conservation of wildlife

- Visitor safety

- Quality experience for visitors

- Pollution control

Note that the rules below only apply to the use of drones for recreational purposes. If the project is in any way commercial (filmmaking, advertising, research, etc.), an appropriate permit must be applied for through Vatnajökull National Park’s website. If the applicant is in doubt as to which category the project falls into, it is best to e-mail an inquiry to [email protected].

## **General rules regarding the use of drones for recreational purposes**

* Flight times should be kept to a minimum and flights should be avoided during the area’s busiest times of day. A drone must never be flown near or over people, unless approval has been obtained (e.g. in organized group tours). Disturbing the experience of tranquillity, guest safety and personal privacy should be avoided.
* It is not allowed to disturb wildlife. If the flight unexpectedly disturbs birds or other wildlife, it must be stopped immediately.
* The conductor of the drone is fully responsible for the use of the drone. It must not harm people, wildlife, or nature, and it must not leave any permanent traces. If a drone is damaged or crashes, it and all its components must be collected and removed. If it is dangerous to recover a crashed drone, or it cannot be found, it must be reported to the national park.
* Many popular destinations in the national park are close to the national park boundary. The National Park has no jurisdiction outside of them, and therefore it is always the responsibility of the drone operator to check if a permission from landowners is needed.
* In other respects, the flight of unmanned aircraft shall be conducted in accordance with regulation no. 1360/2024, on the operation of remotely piloted aircraft and instructions on the Icelandic Transport Authority’ website.
* A poster with the general rules of the Icelandic Transport Authority.

## Regional conditions

In a few parts of the national park, in addition to the general rules of the Icelandic Transport Authority, the site-specific conditions explained below apply.

![Helga Hvanndal](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2Ff89799b6-e095-4f29-9547-65f9f71090f5_askja%2Bog%2B%25C3%25B6skjuvatn_helga%2Bhvanndal_portrait.png%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfaces%252Cedges%26fit%3Dcrop%26w%3D800%26h%3D1200&w=3840&q=80)

Image: Helga Hvanndal

## Category I: Areas where the use of drones is prohibited due to the experience of tranquillity and/or bird protection

In the following areas, flying drones for recreational purposes is prohibited:

* Skaftafell (Svartifoss, Skaftafellsheiði, Morsárdalur, Kjós and Skaftafellsfjöll, etc.) – for bird protection, safety and to ensure a quality visitor experience. The only place in Skaftafell where drones are allowed, is in front of Skaftafellsjökull (see category III).
* Jökulsárlón and Fellsfjara. From April 15th to July 15th – due to bird protection. See category IV for flights outside this time of the year.
* Jökulsárgljúfur (including Ásbyrgi, Vesturdalur, Hljóðaklettar, Hafragilsfoss, etc.) – for bird protection, safety and to ensure a quality visitor experience. It is permitted to fly drones at Dettifoss on the west side of the river (see category V).
* Askja - To ensure a quality visitor experience.

![](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2F570528ba-6dfd-4b8a-8f77-bb428b1ccbfc_Lakag%25C3%25ADgar_portrait.png%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfaces%252Cedges%26fit%3Dcrop%26w%3D800%26h%3D1200&w=3840&q=80)

## Category II: Areas where the use of drones is subject to the verbal permission of rangers

In the following areas, rangers can give verbal permission for the use of drones. The conditions for a permit are based on an assessment of the state of the ecosystem and/or a specific time of day when there is less tourist traffic than usual (e.g. early morning or late afternoon/evening). The ranger records the granting of the permit on a special form. This applies in the following areas:

1. Service area at Drekagil huts
2. Herðubreiðarlindir
3. Hvannalindir
4. Kverkjökull
5. Service area at Sigurðarskáli hut
6. Service area at Snæfell hut
7. Eldgjá
8. Laki

![](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2FZoJi1x5LeNNTwqrr_dr%25C3%25B3nakortSkaftafell31.jpeg%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfaces%252Cedges%26fit%3Dcrop%26w%3D800%26h%3D565.7256914741945&w=3840&q=80)

## Category III: Skaftafellsjökull - Restrictions and permissions

The use of drones in front of Skaftafellsjökull is permitted at certain times of the day, but elsewhere in Skaftafell (Svartifoss, Skaftafellsheiði, Morsárdalur, Kjós and Skaftafellsfjöll, etc.) the use of drones for recreational purposes is prohibited (see red shaded areas on the map) - for bird protection, safety and to ensure a quality visitor experience.

The use of drones is permitted at Skaftafellsjökull at the following times of the day, which vary depending on the time of year and the sunrise/sunset:

* May 1st to September 15th - before 09:00 or after 18:00
* September 15th to October 31st - before 10:00 or after 17:00
* November 1st to February 29th - before 11:00 or after 15:00
* March 1st to April 30th - before: 10:00 or after 17:00

![](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2F424b956e-8b6f-4b60-8cc0-234df0eb6d90_dr%25C3%25B3nakort_jokulsarlon2.jpeg%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfaces%252Cedges%26fit%3Dcrop%26w%3D800%26h%3D1131.2903225806451&w=3840&q=80)

## Category IV: Jökulsárlón and Fellsfjara - Restrictions and permissions

**Drones are not allowed between April 15th and July 15th due to bird protection.**

At other times of the year, time of day restrictions apply, which vary depending on the time of year and the sunrise/sunset.

**During the following times of the day, drones can be used in the area (see map to the side) and a special permit not needed:**

* July 15th to September 21st – before 09:00 or after 18:00.
* September 22nd to October 21st – before 10:00 or after 17:00.
* October 22ndto November 21st – before 11:00 or after 15:00.
* November 22nd to January 21st – before 12:00 or after 14:00.
* January 22nd to February 21st – before 11:00 or after 15:00.
* February 22nd to March 21st – before 10:00 or after 17:00.
* March 22nd to April 15th – before 09:00 or after 18:00.

Helicopter traffic can always be expected in the Jökulsárlón area. Remotely controlled aircraft must always give way to other air traffic, cf. regulation no. 990/2017.

There is generally a loft of visitor traffic in the red shaded area on the map on the right. The use of drones in that area is therefore always prohibited according to regulation no. 990/2017.

![](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2FZj4Q5EFLKBtrWx0d_dr%25C3%25B3nakortdettifoss2.jpeg%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfaces%252Cedges%26fit%3Dcrop%26w%3D800%26h%3D1131.2903225806451&w=3840&q=80)

## Category V: Dettifoss (western side) - Restrictions and permissions

The use of drones at Dettifoss on the western side of the river is permitted at certain times of the day, but elsewhere in Jökulsárgljúfur the use of drones for recreational purposes is not permitted.

The use of drones is permitted at the following times of the day, which vary depending on the time of year and the sunrise/sunset:1. mars til 30. apríl – fyrir kl. 10:00 eða eftir kl. 16:00.

* March 1st to April 30th – before 10:00 or after 16:00.
* May 1st to August 31st – before 8:00 or after 18:00.
* September 1st to October 31st – before 10:00 or after 16:00
* November 1st to February 29th – no time-of-day restrictions, but general conditions apply.

Within the park boundaries at Dettifoss, you may only fly over the canyon itself. Due to bird protection, it is not allowed to fly further north than the northernmost viewing platform west of Dettifoss (where the red shaded area on the map begins). The eastern bank of Jökulsá (shaded green) and south of Dettifoss is privately owned land. On the footpaths to the waterfalls, general conditions for drone flight apply, including a ban on flying over crowds.

Attention is drawn to the fact that in the case of the use of drones in search or rescue operations, the police and rescue teams do not need to obtain a permit for their work. However, it is requested that the staff of the national park be notified of such, as the case may be, afterwards.

# Payment of camping and regional fees

Parka Buisness Solution ehf is the National Park service provider for online paymens for the campground in Ásbyrgi and for the regional fees (collected at parking lots) in Skaftafell and at Jökulsárlón.

![](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2F3c796d69-440d-4063-bf5a-cd52ae57e4ad_Mosi%2Bme%25C3%25B0%2Bgr%25C3%25B3hirslum_SB.jpg%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfaces%252Cedges%26fit%3Dcrop%26w%3D800%26h%3D529.6875&w=3840&q=80)

## Parka app

In the Parka app, you can pay for the campsite in Ásbyrgi as well for the regional fee (charged at parking lots) in Skaftafell and Jökulsárlón. The campsite fee in Skaftafell is paid on site.

Click here to get the Parka app

![](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2Fbd163170-cd9c-4ceb-b042-e0ecb45cf564_%25C3%2596r%25C3%25A6faj%25C3%25B6kull_Skaftafell_19_E%25C3%259EG.jpg%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfaces%252Cedges%26fit%3Dcrop%26w%3D800%26h%3D600&w=3840&q=80)

## Regional fee in Skaftafell

The regional fee (collected at the parking lot) in Skaftafelli is valid until midnight on the day it is paid. During that period, you can drive in and out of the area at will. A 50% discount is given on the regional fee if another fee-based service area of Vatnajökull National Park has been previously visited within the same date as the full fee was paid.

Click here to pay the regional fee in Skaftafell

![](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2F649263ad-350d-4eb2-ad70-0166d24c29cd_j%25C3%25B6kuls%25C3%25A1rl%25C3%25B3n_22_yvon-hoogers_unsplash.jpg%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfaces%252Cedges%26fit%3Dcrop%26w%3D800%26h%3D533.2859680284191&w=3840&q=80)

## Regional fee at Jökulsárlón

The regional fee (collected at the parking lot) at Jökulsárlón is valid until midnight on the day it is paid. During that period, you can drive in and out of the area at will. A 50% discount is given on the regional fee if another fee-based service area of Vatnajökull National Park has been previously visited within the same date as the full fee was paid.

Click here to pay the regional fee at Jökulsárlón

![](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2F00b2b1a6-d7ab-40f5-85d7-7a96e69fe26f_holtas%25C3%25B3ley.jpg%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfaces%252Cedges%26fit%3Dcrop%26w%3D800%26h%3D600&w=3840&q=80)

## P-card, parking card for persons with reduced mobility

Holders of parking cards (P-sign) for persons with reduced mobility can request to be exempted from the collection of the regional fee. A request that the registration number of a vehicle (with which the respective P-sign holder travels) should be included in the list of exemptions should normally be sent by e-mail to [email protected] **in good time before the vehicle is driven to the area**. It should be noted that a P-sign is the reason for the request. Requests are processed during office hours and an answer confirming the registration is sent to the person by e-mail.

![Tjaldsvæði, Ásbyrgi](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2Fc761fe38-da03-4cd4-8e85-70ff4c521257_svaedi-jokulsargljufur-tjaldsvaedi-asbyrgi.jfif%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfaces%252Cedges%26fit%3Dcrop%26w%3D800%26h%3D532.9083665338645&w=3840&q=80)

## Ásbyrgi camping

In Ásbyrgi there is a large camping area with facilities for tents, caravans and mobile homes. It is advisable to book a campsite before arrival due to the popularity of the campsite, and then there is also a greater chance of being able to reserve a pitch with electrical connections.

# [13] https://www.vatnajokulsthjodgardur.is/en/advice--safety/ferdarad

# Travel advice

Nature is the most important thing we have—but do not own. At the very least we are all part of it and have a duty to work together to take care of it. Here is some good travel advice that concerns travellers and nature.

### Weather

Weather forecast from the Icelandic Met Office

### Road conditions

The Icelandic Road Administration

### Safe travel

Submit a travel plan with Safe travel - a project of the Icelandic Association of Search and Rescue

### Tents and overnight stays

Overnight stays outside of defined camping areas are not permitted in the following areas of the National Park:

● Jökulsárgljúfur

● The specifically protected area at Askja

● Kreppulindir

● The lowland areas at Hoffellssvæði and Heinabergssvæði

● Skaftafellsheiði, Bæjarstaðarskógur and Morsárdalur. However, it is permitted to put up tents in the mountains Skaftafellsfjöll above an altitude of 400 metres and in the area at the mouth of the river Kjós. Travellers should get information from the National Park regarding the use of tents in these areas.

Overnight stays outside of marked camping areas in Vatnajökull National Park are otherwise subject to special rules, cf. Article 10 of Regulation 300/2020 regarding Vatnajökull National Park, which the Board establishes in consultation with local councils and publish on the website of the National Park. These rules shall reflect the goals and conditions of the Management and Conservation Plan (which are based on the legal framework of Vatnajökull National Park) and the provisions of the Nature Conservation Act no. 60/2013

### Outdoor recreation

The Natural Park is a place for various forms of outdoor activities and people keep finding more ways to enjoy the outdoors. Hiking routes probably represent one of the oldest forms of outdoorsmanship, and a great many routes are available. There is also a wide-reaching network of roads in the National Park, some of which are paved and accessible to all types of cars, while others are rougher and some can only be navigated in specially equipped cars driven by experienced drivers. In recent years cycling has grown in popularity and a considerable number of visitors choose to travel by bike, for example by crossing the highlands in Summer. The policy of the National Park is to increase the number of defined, marked cycle routes, and roads are of course also accessible to bikes.

● See travel routes

### Seasons

Travelling in Iceland requires preparation because the weather is ever changing and circumstances can vary greatly. Accessibility and services vary greatly by season.

See more about seasons

### Footpaths

Hiking routes of various kinds are available throughout the National Park and the National Park's website and hiking route map are ideal for finding out what trips and routes best suit each individual. It is also possible to get more detailed information in our visitor centres and from rangers.

### Driving on mountain roads

Highland roads are often ill-defined gravel roads with unbridged rivers and some are not suitable or even passable for certain types of vehicles. It is best to seek information from people who know the area before driving on unknown highland roads and it should be noted that it is only permitted to drive on the roads that are indicated on the maps issued by the National Park. Maps in GPS-devices or that are obtained from outside parties can give wrong ideas about clear and open roads.

### Security and precautions

Most of the territory in Vatnajökull National Park is part of the highlands of Iceland where summers are short, the weather is unpredictable and road conditions can deteriorate due to flooding, sandstorms and even snow at any time of the year. We therefore recommend the following tools and actions:

● Weather: Get information about the weather and weather forecasts from the Icelandic Meteorological Office at vedur.is

● Roads: Find out about road conditions and whether roads are open or closed from the Icelandic Road and Coastal Administration at vegagerdin.is

● Cautions: The National Park issues special cautions for specific areas online.

● Recording: We recommend recording travel plans on safetravel.is, especially for walkers and cyclists.

● Emergency phone number: 112

## Let us protect nature

Nature is the most important thing we have—but do not own. At the very least we are all part of it and have a duty to work together to take care of it. All of us also have the responsibility to do our best to protect natural and cultural heritage so that future generations can enjoy them in the same way that we do. In the National Park this means, among other things, that:

● we try to avoid disturbing the local wildlife

● we follow hiking routes where they are marked and/or clearly defined

● we do not build cairns nor add stones to older cairns

● we do not remove nor disarrange stones, plants or other items that form part of natural and cultural heritage

● we keep dogs on a leash

● we do not light open fires

● we do not litter, nor burn or bury waste

● we sort our waste and dispose of it in appropriate locations

● we follow rules about drone use

Jökulsárlón

Fjallsárón is a glacier lagoon in front of Fjallsjökull located on Breiðamerkursandur 10 kilometers from Jökulsárlón. Fjallsárlón offers a unique view over a glacier lagoon, birdlife and glacial landscapes, it is popular among travelers that want to enjoy nature with less crowds. Next to the car park guided tours are available, toilets and a cafeteria.

# Glacier trail - Visitor trail

Skaftafell

The walk along Jökulslóð is like a living history lesson about the retreat of a glacier. The landscapes shaped by glacier are prominent on the route, such as glacier gardens and kettle holes, and you can see the progress of vegetation at different levels. The trail is part of the Melting Glaciers project. Along the way, there are numbered markers that refer to the interpretation that can be found electronically below.

Vegalengd

4,1 km

Áætlaður tími

1-1,5 klst

Erfiðleikastig

Auðveld

Hækkun

80 m

Tegund

Hringleið

Upphafsstaður

Austan við Skaftafellsstofu

About trail difficulty

The trail starts next to Skaftafell Visitor Centre and is well indicated. It is paved half the way, while the latter half has a fairly comfortable gravel surface. The trail ends in close proximity of Skaftafellsjökull glacier and you will get a good view of this impressive glacier. Those who wish get closer can do so but are encouraged to follow instructions on a warning sign at the trail's end. When heading back to the visitor centre it is possible to take an alternative route by turning left on the crossroads that you arrived at some 300 m before the end point (well indicated with signs).

# Gljúfrastofa Visitor Centre

Jökulsárgljúfur

Gljúfrastofa in Ásbyrgi is a visitor centre for Vatnajökull National Park in Jökulsárgljúfur.

### Opening hours

January 2nd – April 30th: 11:00–15:00 Monday to Friday

May 1st – May 31th: 10:00–17:00 every day

June 1th – August 31st: 9:00–18:00 every day

September 1st – October 31st: 10:00–17:00 every day

November 1st – December 20th: 11:00–15:00 Monday to Friday

Phone

+354 470 7100

Email

[email protected]

Ásbyrgi Visitor Centre hosts a tourist information centre, an exhibit about the Jökulsárgljúfur canyon area and a small souvenir shop with emphasis on books and local handicraft. It is conveniently located at the mouth of the Ásbyrgi canyon which is also the location of the majority of Ásbyrgi’s and Jökulsárgljúfur’s trailheads. Restrooms are located inside the visitor centre and are accessible during opening hours only.

Ásbyrgi Visitor Centre is a part of the Arctic Coast Way; a 900 km long touring route that threads through the coastline of northern Iceland.

There's a charging station on the parking lot in front the visitor centre for electric and hybrid cars.

## Campsite

There is a large campsite in Ásbyrgi with facilities for tents, caravans and mobile homes. There's also a beautiful campsite in Vesturdalur that is only intended for tents and there is neither electricity nor hot water.

# Breiðamerkursandur

Breiðamerkursandur became a part of Vatnajökull National Park in 2017. It is located within the municipality of Hornafjörður, west of the town Höfn. Jökulsárlón is one of the main destinations of Breiðamerkursandur along with Fjallsárlón.

Parking lot

WC

Hiking

No overnight stays

## Nature

Breiðamerkursandur, the outwash plain south of Breiðamerkurjökull, displays some of Iceland’s most accessible landscapes formed by glaciers. The area has been changing rapidly, and in a rather short time it is possible to study the scenario of these changes in the landscape due to the fast retreat of the glaciers. The fastest changes can be seen at Breiðamerkurjökull, especially at the front of the glacier where it calves into Jökulsárlón. The icebergs on Jökulsárlón are some of the main attractions in the area. Breiðamerkursandur is also an important nesting grounds for various bird species, such as the Great skua and the Barnacle goose. Scientific research is conducted in these ever-changing surroundings, both on the landscape as well as on the colonization of the biosphere.

The area has a diverse fauna, and several reindeer herds stay on the eastern side of Breiðamerkursandur during the winter. Please take caution when driving through the area, as they tend to run across the road.

## History

Breiðamerkursandur is between the rivers Kvíá and Fellsá and was formerly called Breiðársandur after the farm Breiðá. Place names in the area provide evidence of habitation from the first centuries of the settlement of Iceland. At the time, the glaciers were smaller, but later the plain was covered with vegetation. In the 13th century, the climate began to cool, and as a result, glaciers advanced. Breiðamerkurjökull advanced until the 18th and 19th centuries. Only about 250 metres separated the glacier and the sea where the distance between them was shortest. Massive changes in landscape have taken place on Breiðamerkursandur after the glacier began to retreat in the beginning of the 20th century. Lakes began to appear in the 1930s as the glaciers retreated. The largest lakes are Jökulsárlón, Breiðárlón and Fjallsárlón. Each of them is fed by a calving glacier, but with the continued retreat of the glaciers, the lakes keep growing. The land that appears with the retreat is characterized by the sediments formed under the glacier.

According to Landnáma, *(the Book of Settlements)*, the settlement of Breiðamerkursandur began almost 1100 years ago, almost immediately after Iceland’s settlement began. At that time, Ari Fróði’s Íslendingabók *(the Book of Icelanders)*, tells us that the land was forested from the mountains and all the way to the shore. It is hard to imagine farming on Breiðamerkursandur as we know it today, let alone building a farm in the middle of the plain. The conditions have changed drastically, and the nature is more difficult for farming than it was in the first centuries of settlement.

# Svartifoss

Skaftafell

It is safe to say that Svartifoss is one of Iceland’s best-known waterfalls, thanks to the way in which it is beautifully framed by unusually symmetric, thick basalt columns. These columns were formed about 300,000 years ago, when lava flowed down the Skaftafellsheiði plain and filled an old riverbed. As it slowly cooled down, the rock contracted and then split into columns that stand perpendicular to the cooling surface.

## Accomodation

Snæfellsskáli cabin is at Snæfell and acommodates around 45 people. There is also a campsite, flush toilets and showers. There are several cabins in the nature reserve at Lónsöræfi and adjacent areas.

![](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2Fd53d0cd6-5da8-4c8d-b26b-d5d703004598_Sn%25C3%25A6fellssk%25C3%25A1li%2BH%25C3%2581%2B2022.jpg%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfaces%252Cedges%26fit%3Dcrop%26w%3D510%26h%3D756&w=1080&q=80)

## Accessibility

### Accessibility in Snæfellsstofa

Snæfellsstofa Visitor Centre is accessible for individuals with reduced mobility. A reserved parking space is in front of the visitor centre and a clear pathway is from there to the entrance. The front door opens automatically and there is plenty of space to navigate indoors. The visitor centre has an accessible bathroom available to guests. Employees have received instructions in welcoming guests with reduced mobility and are always ready to help. Attached are images the showcase the accessibility in Snæfellsstofa (Pictured by Tinna Hallgrímsdóttir).

# World Heritage Site

To be included in the UNESCO World Heritage List, a place or phenomenon must have what is called a unique value on a global scale. Vatnajökull National Park was inscribed as a World Heritage Site in 2019 due an outstanding example representing major stages of earth's history, including the record of life, significant on-going geological processes in the development of landforms, or significant geomorphic or physiographic features.

![](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2Ff4bb8f47-ff13-45a0-89e0-da074b80d81a_Lambagras_SG%25C3%259E_09.jpg%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfaces%252Cedges%26fit%3Dcrop%26w%3D1640%26h%3D820&w=3840&q=80)

The United Nations Educational, Scientific and Cultural Organization (UNESCO) seeks to encourage the identification, protection and preservation of cultural and natural heritage around the world considered to be of outstanding value to humanity. This is embodied in an international treaty called the Convention concerning the Protection of the World Cultural and Natural Heritage, adopted by UNESCO in 1972. Cultural heritage refers to monuments, groups of buildings and sites with historical, aesthetic, archaeological, scientific, ethnological or anthropological value. Natural heritage refers to outstanding physical, biological and geological formations, habitats of threatened species of animals and plants and areas with scientific, conservation or aesthetic value

Heritage is our legacy from the past, what we live with today, and what we pass on to future generations. Our cultural and natural heritage are both irreplaceable sources of life and inspiration. They are our touchstones, our points of reference, our identity. What makes the concept of World Heritage exceptional is its universal application. World Heritage sites belong to all the peoples of the world, irrespective of the territory on which they are located.

![](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2F664b7eb5-aebe-4f14-8089-aaefd83468e2_Kverkj%25C3%25B6kull_SG%25C3%259E_09.jpg%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfaces%252Cedges%26fit%3Dcrop%26w%3D1200%26h%3D1200&w=3840&q=80)

## Dynamic Nature of Fire and Ice

Vatnajökull National Park was inscribed on the World Heritage list under natural criterion (viii). That means that the area qualifies as an outstanding example representing major stages of earth's history, including the record of life, significant on-going geological processes in the development of landforms, or significant geomorphic or physiographic features.

### Unique nature

The coexistence and ongoing interaction of an active oceanic rift on land, a mantle plume, the atmosphere and an ice cap, which has varied in size and extent over the past 2.8 million years, make the property unique in a global context. Earth system interactions are constantly building and reshaping the property, creating remarkably diverse landscapes and a wide variety of tectonic, volcanic and glaciovolcanic features. Especially interesting and unique in this regard are the basaltic lava shields (Iceland shields), volcanic fissures and cone rows, vast flood lavas, and features of ice dominant glacio-volcanism, such as tuyas and tindar. Interestingly, the well exposed volcanic features of the property have been used as analogues for similar features on the planet Mars. Geothermal heat and subglacial eruptions produce meltwater and jökulhlaups that maintain globally unique sandur plains, to the north and south of the Vatnajökull ice cap, as well as rapidly evolving canyons. In addition, the property contains a dynamic array of glacial- and geomorphological features, created by expanding or retreating glaciers responding to changes in climate. These features can be easily accessed and explored at the snouts of Vatnajökull’s many outlet glaciers and their forelands, especially in the southern lowlands, making the property a flagship glacial research location.

### Fire and Ice

This iconic volcanic region covers an area of over 1,400,000 ha, nearly 14% of Iceland's territory. It numbers ten central volcanoes, eight of which are subglacial. Two of these are among the most active in Iceland. The interaction between volcanoes and the rifts that underlie the Vatnajökull ice cap takes many forms, the most spectacular of which is the *jökulhlaup* – a sudden flood caused by the breach of the margin of a glacier during an eruption. This recurrent phenomenon has led to the emergence of unique sandur plains, river systems and rapidly evolving canyons. Volcanic areas are home to endemic groundwater fauna that has survived the Ice Age.

![Hvannadalshnjúkur séður frá Skaftafellsheiði (mynd: Magnús Guðmundsson)](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2Fb3fb4c07-197d-4bb2-8c47-fc23b4fc19c7_Hvannadalshnj%25C3%25BAkur%2Bfr%25C3%25A1%2BSkaftafellshei%25C3%25B0i_22_MG.jpg%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfaces%252Cedges%26fit%3Dcrop%26w%3D1640%26h%3D739&w=3840&q=80)

## The Nomination Process

The Icelandic Government’s proposal for the inscription of Vatnajökull National Park on the World Heritage List signed by Guðmundur Ingi Guðbrandsson, Minister for the Environment and Natural Resources and Lilja Alfreðsdóttir, Minister of Education, Science and Culture. The proposal was presented to the office of the World Heritage Convention in Paris in January 2018. Preparation of the nomination started in 2016 and the nomination document was prepared by Vatnajökull National Park as commissioned by the Ministry for the Environment and Natural Resources and the Ministry of Education, Science and Culture. A steering group was set up by the ministries to oversee and manage the nomination process.

On the 5th of July 2019 Vatnajökull National Park was inscribed on UNESCO’s list of World Heritage Sites. The decision, which was taken at a meeting of the World Heritage Committee in Baku, Azerbaijan confirmed that the natural environment of the Park and the Nature Reserve at Lónsöræfi have a unique value to humankind.

## Management and protection requirements

The government agency Vatnajökull National Park (Vatnajökulsþjóðgarður) is the primary state agency responsible for implementing the park legislation, and is an effective organization, supported at all levels by the Icelandic government, local municipalities and businesses. There is mature governance in place together with experienced staff responsible for management employed on a long-term basis, including a strong complement of permanent and temporary staff.

Recommends the State Party address the following, to maintain and strengthen the protection and management of the property:

1. Complete, in a timely manner, the current revision of the management plan for Vatnajökull National Park, ensuring it integrates fully all areas included in the property,
2. Seek to complete integration of the Herðubreiðarlindir and Lónsöræfi Nature Reserves into Vatnajökull National Park in order to facilitate cohesive management of the whole property,
3. Make available additional staff resources, including both field staff and administrative support, to ensure the effective protection and management of the property, in view of the recent areas that were added to Vatnajökull National Park, and the recorded rapid recent increase in visitation to the property,
4. Put in place adequate visitor facilities in the heavily visited areas around the Jökulsárlón Lagoon in the south of the property, and also at the Dettifoss Waterfall to the north of the property,
5. Adopt and implement effective certification for commercial operators and guides operating in the property, and
6. Take additional measures to discourage illegal off-road driving by visitors, and to rehabilitate any areas affected adversely by these and other visitor uses.

# [26] https://www.vatnajokulsthjodgardur.is/en/melting-glaciers/um-horfandi-jokla

# Melting glaciers

Glaciers cover around 10% of Iceland. After 1890, most glaciers began to retreat and since the year 2000 the outlet glaciers have retreated exceptionally fast, and their mass loss is among the highest recorded in the world. Due to climate change the glaciers will continue to melt and retreat and they could lose half of their volume by 2100. After 200 years, only small ice caps will remain on the highest mountains.

![](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2Fdda57684-1618-426c-9463-2034b59e4c22_3_10b_skridjokull_sg_HJ.jpg%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfaces%252Cedges%26fit%3Dcrop%26w%3D1640%26h%3D820&w=3840&q=80)

![](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2Fbff27b9e-bc51-45ef-b113-1622337e5bbe_H%25C3%25B6rfandi%2Bj%25C3%25B6klar%2Bt%25C3%25A1kn.png%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfaces%252Cedges%26fit%3Dcrop%26w%3D800%26h%3D565.7924743443558&w=3840&q=80)

## Melting glaciers - the project

This website is part of the project Icelandic Glaciers – a natural laboratory to study climate change, Melting Glaciers for short. The Ministry for the Environment and Natural Resources has appointed the Vatnajökull National Park in cooperation with the Icelandic Meteorological Office to implement the project, in collaboration with the South East Iceland Nature Research Center and the Institute of Earth Sciences of the University of Iceland. The goal of the project, which is a part of the government’s climate change agenda, is to increase awareness about climate change and the associated consequences for glaciers in Iceland and elsewhere.

## News of Icelandic glaciers

A newsletter is published yearly with updates from the glaciers in Iceland that have retreated rapidly for a quarter of a century, and glacier downwasting is one of the most obvious consequences of a warming climate in the country. In this newsletter, glacier changes since about 1900 are described briefly along with the results of monitoring of glacier termini, glacier mass balance and crustal movements induced by glacier changes in recent years. Updated maps of glacier outlines in the fall of 2021 and updated mass balance time series are presented.

## Glaciers & climate change

Global surface temperatures increased by 0.8°C on average during the 20th century and twice as much in the Arctic and sub-polar areas.

More information about climate change and glaciers

## Glaciology

Glacial ice s composed of ice crystals, snow, air, water and sediments. Glaciers form when more snow accumulates over the year than melts during the summer. Here you can learn how glaciers form and everything from crevasses to subglacial eruptions including facts about Vatnajökull glacier.

# The unique nature of the National Park

The natural and cultural history of the Vatnajökull National Park is unique on a global level. The nature there is shaped by heavy volcanic activity in the Mid-Atlantic Ridge and a climate defined by a confluence of warm and cold currents in the sea and air.

![](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2Ff07a66dd-9533-46ef-a084-9f26a6211f6d_esjufjoll_reedit_drone-0586%2B%25281%2529%25C3%259Eorsteinn%2BRoy.jpg%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfaces%252Cedges%26fit%3Dcrop%26w%3D1640%26h%3D820&w=3840&q=80)

![](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2Fd3b5e179-2aaf-4cbd-bda5-3a5829dd8f25_25.png%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfaces%252Cedges%26fit%3Dcrop%26w%3D660%26h%3D660&w=3840&q=80)

![](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2Ffdafe5f8-8f09-4929-a8fe-da121b98fdc7_Kverkfj%25C3%25B6ll%2Bh%25C3%25A1hiti_SRR.jpg%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfaces%252Cedges%26fit%3Dcrop%26w%3D533%26h%3D533&w=3840&q=80)

## Fire & Ice

Vatnajökull National Park is unique on a global level due to its geological history, which has been largely written by the protracted conflict between fire and ice. Many places can boast of volcanoes or glaciers, and some even have both, but nowhere else can you witness the struggle that rages between plate boundaries, mantle plume and plateau glaciers like in Vatnajökull National Park. The interaction between these phenomena and other land-shaping factors has resulted in the creation of a natural environment that is ever-changing and of a more diverse character than any other defined area in the world. Vatnajökull National Park offers the opportunity to experience the shaping of the earth in real time.

## What is a national park?

National parks are most commonly large areas that are mostly untouched or only slightly impacted by human activity, with unique features as regards nature, ecosystems, landscape and geological or cultural heritage. When an area is declared a national park a decision is made to protect that area for the long term, and that its natural and cultural heritage shall be taken into consideration in all construction, organisation and activities that take place there. National parks belong to the people and offer locations for outdoor activities, education and experiences. In order to protect ecosystems, locations to experience nature, and cultural heritage sites, certain infrastructure and organisation is required, and sometimes it is necessary to limit access.

## UNESCO World Heritage List

Vatnajökull National Park was included on the UNESCO World Heritage List on 5 July 2019 on the basis of its unique natural characteristics.

Learn more about World Heritage

## More about the National Park's activities

### Policy

Our ambitions are towards the conservation of unique nature and cultural heritage, which has been shaped by the interaction between fire and ice.

### Management and Conservation Plan

The plan is based on proposals from local councils and the Board of Directors and formulated in consultation with interested parties. The objective of the plan is to tread a path of balance between conservation and land use.

### Policy formulation & participation

Various policies have been established regarding the activities in Vatnajökull National Park, and more are being formulated.

The landscape of the National Park is shaped by the synergy between volcanoes and glaciers. It bears witness to lava flows, geothermal activity, hyaloclastite ridges and table mountains, terminal moraines, ice dammed lakes and glacial plains, with the ice sheet looming above. At the south part of Vatnajökull there are coastal areas and open sea.

# Work sites

At Vatnajökull National Park, there is a cohesive group of employees with diverse experience, who carry out a variety of tasks related to nature conservation and service to the visitors of the national park.

![](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2F2321c3b6-4f4f-4d7d-8207-a54eb9aedf03_Landv%25C3%25B6r%25C3%25B0ur_Laki2_2021_%25C3%258Db%25C3%25AD.jpg%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfaces%252Cedges%26fit%3Dcrop%26w%3D1640%26h%3D820&w=3840&q=80)

The National Park normally employs over 30 people, but during the summer the number of employees exceeds 100. The National Park currently operates five visitor centers. They are: Gljúfrastofa in Ásbyrgi, Gígur visitor centre at Mývatn, Snæfellsstofa in Skriðuklaustur, Skaftafellsstofa in Skaftafell and Skaftárstofa in Kirkjubæjarklaustur, which is run in housing and in cooperation with Skaftárhrepp, until a new one is built.

During the summer, the activities of the national park extend widely throughout the park, but in the winter, when the highlands are closed, it is limited to fewer places. The map here shows the national park's ranger stations and offices. Ranger stations are marked with orange boxes. The National Park legal domicile is in Höfn. Various support services are provided from offices in Höfn, Fellabær and Akureyri. The power behind the National Park lies not only in the unique nature, but also in the energetic and happy staff.

## Ranger stations

Ranger stations are the work places of rangers and also centers for information and education in the form of walking tours or other media depending on the situation.

Ranger stations are located where they are guaranteed to serve their purpose and reach visitors passing through the area. From there, rangers carry out daily patrols within the National Park and in the protected areas under its care. Ranger stations can be operated in collaboration with several parties, and where possible, attempts are made to use both housing options and other services that are already available.

The role of rangers and other staff may have different priorities between ranger stations. The same applies to the facilities of the employees at each location.

# Pricelist

Here is the pricelist that applies in Vatnajökull National Park regarding fees for camping, overnighting in huts, regional fees for Skaftafell and Jökulsárlón, permit processing fees and other fees..

![](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2F258322ae-39ac-46ef-a2b9-3bf90c83c246_Tjald%2Bog%2Bgestir%2B%25C3%25AD%2BSkaftafelli_22_GSVI.jpg%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfocalpoint%26fit%3Dcrop%26fp-x%3D0.50%26fp-y%3D0.62%26w%3D1640%26h%3D820&w=3840&q=80)

## Payment for camping and area fees

Parka Buisness Solution ehf is the National Park service provider for online paymens for the campground in Ásbyrgi and for the regional fees (collected at parking lots) in Skaftafell and at Jökulsárlón.

Click here for further information about payments

## **Campsites in Ásbyrgi and Skaftafell**

The fee is based on accommodation for one person for one night. Accommodation tax is included in the fee.

* Included in the campsite fee is access to a shower, washing machine and dryer (dryer is in Skaftafell).
* Senior citizens (67 years and older) and disabled persons are given a discount on accommodation fees.
* Access for children aged 16 and under accompanied by an adult to the campsite is free of charge.  
    
  \*Note: The electrical connection is only intended for electrical devices within the area. It is not permitted to use the connection to charge vehicles, the systems are neither powerful nor safe enough for such charging.

![](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2F19de41a4-3c75-4ca0-954b-df7e0c01ca99_2021%2B09%2B06%2B-%2Bfleiri%2Bgistinaetur%2Bi%2Basbyrgi%2Ben%2Bfyrir%2Bcovid-GO.jpg%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfaces%252Cedges%26fit%3Dcrop%26w%3D1200%26h%3D1200&w=3840&q=80)

## Ásbyrgi

ISK 2,800 - General fee per person (17-66 years old) per night

ISK 2,300 - Senior citizens (67 years and older) and disabled per night

Free - Children 16 yrs and under, accompanied by an adult

*A 15% discount on the campsite fee is provided for guests who stay for five consecutive nights or longer.*

ISK 1,500 - Electricity at the campsite, per 24 hours

Take a look at the campsite in Ásbyrgi

![](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2F258322ae-39ac-46ef-a2b9-3bf90c83c246_Tjald%2Bog%2Bgestir%2B%25C3%25AD%2BSkaftafelli_22_GSVI.jpg%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfaces%252Cedges%26fit%3Dcrop%26w%3D1200%26h%3D1200&w=3840&q=80)

## Skaftafell

ISK 2,800 - General fee per person (17-66 years old) per night

ISK 2,300 - Senior citizens (67 years and older) and disabled per night

Free - Children 16 yrs and under, accompanied by an adult

*A 15% discount on the campsite fee is provided for guests who stay for five consecutive nights or longer.*

ISK 1,500 - Electricity at the campsite, per 24 hours

The campsite has 8 pitches, marked with the letters A-H. The pitches are divided into categories according to access to electricity, whether they can be driven into or not, and whether there are clearly designated areas.

**Pitches E, F, G and H**

Driving is prohibited at these pitches

**Pithces B & D**

Access for cars and to electricity – not separate areas

**Pitches A & C**

Access for cars, separate areas and good access to electricity

Take a look at the campsite in Skaftafell

![](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2Fdd8cb43b-6e8f-471a-bf4b-d962ebcd19b0_J%25C3%25B6kuls%25C3%25A1rl%25C3%25B3n_%2Bvetur_20_HS%25C3%259E.jpg%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfaces%252Cedges%26fit%3Dcrop%26w%3D1200%26h%3D1200&w=3840&q=80)

## Regional fee (parking) - Skaftafell and Jökulsárlón

* Category A – Passenger car, 5 or fewer seats 1.040 ISK
* Category B - Passenger car, 6-9 seats 1.350 ISK
* Category C - Bus, 10-21 seats 2.600 ISK
* Category D - Bus, 22-32 seats 4.890 ISK
* Category E - Bus, 33 seats or more 8.840 ISK
* Motorcycle 420 ISK

The regional fee is valid until midnight on the date it is paid. During that period, you can drive in and out of the area freely.

A 50% discount is given on the regional fee if another fee-based service area of Vatnajökull National Park has been previously visited within the same date as the regional fee was paid.

Click here for further information about payment

Collection of area fees is carried out by taking a photo of the license plate of a car that drives into a certain parking lot, and if certain conditions are met, the owner of the vehicle is required to pay the area fee, which can be paid in several ways. You can also pay the zone fee in the payment machines in the parking lots.

![](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2Ff4bb8f47-ff13-45a0-89e0-da074b80d81a_Lambagras_SG%25C3%259E_09.jpg%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfaces%252Cedges%26fit%3Dcrop%26w%3D1200%26h%3D1200&w=3840&q=80)

## P-card, parking card for persons with reduced mobility

Holders of parking cards (P-sign) for persons with reduced mobility can request to be exempted from the collection of the regional fee. A request that the registration number of a vehicle (with which the respective P-sign holder travels) should be included in the list of exemptions should normally be sent by e-mail to [email protected] **in good time before the vehicle is driven to the area**. It should be noted that a P-sign is the reason for the request. Requests are processed during office hours and an answer confirming the registration is sent to the person by e-mail.

## **Blágil & Snæfell - Huts and camping**

The fee is based on accommodation for one person for one night. Accommodation tax is included in the fee.

Senior citizens (67 years and older) and disabled persons are given a 20% discount on accommodation fees.

![Skáli, tjaldsvæði, Blágil](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2Fbda89bc2-9b18-4347-b95d-ec3e640688c7_gisting-skali-blagil.jfif%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfaces%252Cedges%26fit%3Dcrop%26w%3D1200%26h%3D1200&w=3840&q=80)

# [32] https://www.vatnajokulsthjodgardur.is/en/communities--businesses/permits

# Permits

Various activities within Vatnajökull National Park require a permit, such as research, film and advertising production.

![](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2F0602f07a-aa6a-4b3d-89b6-de3182bfeb25_Lj%25C3%25B3smyndari%2BLaki_19_SRR.JPG%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfaces%252Cedges%26fit%3Dcrop%26w%3D1640%26h%3D820&w=3840&q=80)

Vatnajökull National Park's cooperation with the scientific community is both statutory and extremely valuable. Multidisciplinary research is carried out in the dynamic nature of Vatnajökull National Park every year. The National Park is also a popular location for film and commercial shoots, and the use of drones by tourists is increasingly popular.

#### Objectives of the establishment of Vatnajökull National Park according to Article 2. in the national park law is to:

* Protect the nature of the area, such as landscape, biosphere, geological formations and cultural monuments.
* Give the public the opportunity to get to know and enjoy the nature and history of the area.
* Promote research in the area, educate about it and encourage increased public understanding of the quality and uniqueness of the area.
* Strive to strengthen settlements and business activities in the vicinity of the national park, i.a. by encouraging the sustainable use of the quality of the area.

The aforementioned goals are a guiding light in all the work of the national park and strategic planning, i.a. when contracts are made for private business-related activities in the national park. The primary condition is always that the activities comply with these goals.

![](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2FZmBOnpm069VX1edv_Dr%25C3%25B3ni_canvamyndabanki.png%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfaces%252Cedges%26fit%3Dcrop%26w%3D800%26h%3D450&w=3840&q=80)

## Drone rules - recreational use

In general, the use of drones for recreational purposes is allowed in Vatnajökull National Park, but regional restrictions apply in some areas. The general rules of the Icelandic Transport Authority must be followed, but the rules of Vatnajökull National Park (general and regional) below also apply.

CLICK HERE FOR RULES ON FLYING DRONES IN RECREATIONAL USE

## Commercial filming permit

Those who intend to do filmmaking, advertising or other such activities within the national park fill out an application for a permit in the service portal (link to the application form further down the page).   
A permit must be applied for at least 30 days in advance. Film and advertising licenses are charged according to Vatnajökull National Park's price list.

## Events

Here you can apply for permission to hold events other than those discussed above. Examples of events can be: sports tournaments, gatherings (weddings, birthdays, family reunions, etc.), concerts, film screenings, etc. An event permit must be applied for as far in advance as the applicant can.

# [33] https://www.vatnajokulsthjodgardur.is/advaranir/framhlaup-er-hafid-i-dyngjujokli

# Framhlaup í Dyngjujökli

Samkvæmt GPS-hraðamælingum Jarðvísindastofnunar Háskólans er framhlaup í Dyngjujökli. Hér að neðan er samantekt frá Veðurstofu Íslands.

19. nóvember 2025, kl. 16:37

Framhlaup er hafið í Dyngjujökli | Fréttir | Veðurstofa Íslands

* Fólki er bent á að gæta sérstakrar varúðar á ferðalögum á Dyngjujökli þar sem sprungumyndun er líkleg á svæðum sem hafa verið greiðfær og ósprungin í 20 ár.
* Dyngjujökull er þekktur framhlaupsjökull og að jafnaði líða 20 til 30 ár á milli framhlaupa.
* Framhlaup er óregla í hreyfingu jökla sem felur í sér tímabundna og mikla hröðun á skriði, oft tífalt, hundraðfalt eða meira, og jökullinn springur upp á stórum svæðum.
* Umfang framhlaupsins er enn óljóst en hægt er að miða við svæðið sem síðasta framhlaup náði til.
* Afrennsli eykst í framhlaupum og vatn sprettur fram á stærra svæði en venjulega auk þess sem aurburður í ám vex margfalt.
* Innlendar stofnanir fylgjast með þróuninni í samstarfi við erlenda rannsóknaraðila.

Svæðið sem síðasta framhlaup náði til og hafa má gróflega til hliðsjónar varðandi líklegt umfang hlaupsins sem nú er hafið, er sýnt á kortinu.

![](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2FaR3wv2GnmrmGqC0L_Dyngjujokull_overview_20251118.jpg%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfaces%252Cedges%26fit%3Dcrop%26w%3D2000%26h%3D2400&w=3840&q=80)

# [35] https://www.vatnajokulsthjodgardur.is/frettir/opinn-fundur-um-framtidaruppbyggingu-vid-jokulsarlon

# Opinn fundur um framtíðaruppbyggingu við Jökulsárlón

Fundurinn verður haldinn á Hrollaugsstöðum 24. júní næstkomandi klukkan 17:00

16. júní 2026

![](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2FajFXE41P9HI4Uk18_J%25C3%25B6kuls%25C3%25A1rl%25C3%25B3n_canva.png%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfaces%252Cedges%26fit%3Dcrop%26w%3D1640%26h%3D820&w=3840&q=80)

Náttúruverndarstofnun, í samstarfi við Umhverfis-, orku-og loftslagsráðuneytið og Sveitarfélagið Hornafjörð, boðar opinn fund um málefni framtíðaruppbyggingar við Jökulsárlón, þann 24. júní n.k..

Á fundinum munu fulltrúar stýrihóps um framtíðaruppbyggingu við Jökulsárlón fara yfir þá vinnu sem unnin hefur verið og kynna næstu skref í ferlinu. Jafnframt gefst íbúum og öðrum hagsmunaaðilum kostur á að koma sjónarmiðum sínum og ábendingum á framfæri. Markmið fundarins er að eiga milliliðalaust samtal um framhald málsins og tryggja að sjónarmið nærsamfélagsins nýtist við áframhaldandi mótun verkefnisins.

Staðsetning: Hrollaugsstaðir í Suðursveit

Tími: 17:00-19:00

Boðið verður upp á veitingar.

Viðburður á facebook síðu Vatnajökulsþjóðgarðs

# [36] https://www.vatnajokulsthjodgardur.is/frettir/oryggi-gesta-i-fyrirrumi-breytt-a%C3%B0gengi-a%C3%B0-detti

# Öryggi gesta í fyrirrúmi - breytt aðgengi að Dettifossi vestan ár

28. maí 2026

![](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2Ff5f4c113-6c0e-40af-9c54-31a8ff346395_dettifoss-vestan-2019-GO.jpg%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfaces%252Cedges%26fit%3Dcrop%26w%3D1640%26h%3D820&w=3840&q=80)

Dettifoss / Guðmundur Ögmundsson

Í kjölfar mikilla rigninga í byrjun sumars árið 2025 féll bergskriða við Dettifoss. Víða komu sprungur í ljós á gljúfurbarmi og í kjölfarið var svæðinu við Fosshvamm lokað í öryggisskyni á meðan frekari athuganir fóru fram. Við rannsóknir sérfræðinga á svæðinu kom í ljós að víðfeðmt sprungunet leynist víða undir jarðveginum og sprungur gera engan greinarmun á göngustígum, útsýnispöllum eða klettum.

Ljóst er að veðrun er mikil á svæðinu, hvort sem það er í formi fossúðans, sem baðar nærsvæði fossins upp úr vatni hvern dag eða árviss átök frosts og þýðu þegar veturinn færist yfir. Það er í eðli gljúfra að vera í sífelldri mótun, bæði berghrun og sprungumyndun mála iðnar nýjar myndir í landslagið. Jökulsárgljúfur eru þar engin undantekning, þetta er svæði þar sem náttúran ein ræður ríkjum.

Líf okkar mannfólksins er einnig breytingum háð og við þurfum að aðlaga okkur að náttúrunni og breyttum aðstæðum. Ljóst er að Fosshvammurinn er ekki öruggt svæði lengur og þjóðgarðsvörður verður að grípa til viðeigandi aðgerða til að tryggja öryggi gesta á svæðinu til framtíðar. Því hefur verið tekin sú ákvörðun að Fosshvamminum verði varanlega lokað fyrir allri umferð.

Strax í sumar verða innviðir fjarlægðir af sprungusvæðinu í Fosshvammi og gönguleiðir verða færðar með tilliti til þessara breytinga. Áfram verður unnið að frekari uppbygginu göngupalla norðan við fossinn, þar sem gert verður ráð fyrir hringleið.

Unnið verður að endurheimt vistkerfa í Fosshvammi, sem leiða mun til bættrar gæðaupplifunar gesta með fallegri ásýnd að fossinum.

Skýrt er að aðgengi að Dettifossi mun breytast frá því sem áður var en útsýnið að honum verður áfram til staðar. Hinn forni vinur mun áfram syngja sinn söng og heilla hvern þann sem ber hann augum.

# [37] https://www.vatnajokulsthjodgardur.is/frettir/breytingartillagaausturafrett

# Breytingatillaga að stjórnunar- og verndaráætlun Vatnajökulsþjóðgarðs er varðar svæði sem nefnt er Austurafrétt Bárðdæla

Breytingatillaga hefur verið lögð fram til kynningar næstu 6 vikurnar.

7. maí 2026

![](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2FafyyVsBOoF08xuhe_2026n%25C3%25A1tt%25C3%25BAra.isvefurLANDSCAPE-3-.png%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfaces%252Cedges%26fit%3Dcrop%26w%3D1640%26h%3D820&w=3840&q=80)

Breytingatillaga að stjórnunar- og verndaráætlun Vatnajökulsþjóðgarðs er varðar svæði sem nefnt er Austurafrétt Bárðdæla hefur verið lögð fram til kynningar næstu 6 vikurnar.

Stjórnunar‐ og verndaráætlun fyrir Vatnajökulsþjóðgarð er sett fram í samræmi við ákvæði laga um Vatnajökulsþjóðgarð nr. 60/2007 og reglugerð um Vatnajökulsþjóðgarð nr. 300/2020. Við stækkun þjóðgarðs er heimilt að gera viðauka við stjórnunar og verndaráætlun, sbr. 12. gr. laga um Vatnajökulsþjóðgarð sem segir: „Við stækkun þjóðgarðsins er heimilt að gera viðauka við stjórnunar- og verndaráætlun með lýsingu á mörkum og náttúru og ákvæðum um stjórnun og vernd hins nýja svæðis, án þess að öll áætlunin sé tekin til endurskoðunar. Um málsmeðferð fer samkvæmt ákvæðum 4.–6. mgr. Sameina skal viðaukann við stjórnunar- og verndaráætlun við næstu endurskoðun hennar.“

Hluti af Austurafrétt Bárðdæla var friðlýst sem hluti af Vatnajökulsþjóðgarði í september 2021 með breytingu nr. 1135/2021 á reglugerð nr. 300/2020 um Vatnajökulsþjóðgarð.

Tillaga að breytingu á stjórnunar- og verndaráætlun Vatnajökulsþjóðgarðs varðandi Austurafrétt Bárðdæla var samþykkt til kynningar á fundi svæðisráðs norðursvæðis þann 2.3.2026 og á fundi svæðisstjórnar Vatnajökulsþjóðgarðs þann 22.4.2026.

Framlagða tillögu að breytingu á stjórnunar- og verndaráætlun Vatnajökulsþjóðgarðs og upplýsingar um aðdraganda hennar hér.

Nánari upplýsingar er að fá hjá starfandi þjóðgarðsverði á norðursvæði, Stefaníu Eir Vignisdóttur netfang: [email protected] eða verkefnastjóra, Helgu Árnadóttur netfang: [email protected].

Óskað er eftir athugasemdum frá almenningi við drög að áætluninni en aðkoma hagsmunaaðila er mikilvægur þáttur til að skapa sátt um daglegan rekstur og framtíð friðlýstra svæða.

Opinn kynningarfundur um áætlunina verður haldinn fimmtudaginn 21.maí, kl 17.00, að Kiðagili í Bárðardal.

Athugasemdum skal skilað með tölvupósti til Náttúruverndarstofnunar ([email protected]) eða bréfleiðis með utanáskriftinni: Náttúruverndarstofnun, Austurvegi 4, 860 Hvolsvöllur.

Skilafrestur athugasemda er til og með 18. júní 2026.

# [38] https://www.vatnajokulsthjodgardur.is/frettir/lausstorfadstodarjokulsarlon

# Laus störf: Að­stoð­ar­þjóð­garðs­vörð­ur suð­aust­ur­svæð­is Vatna­jök­uls­þjóð­garðs

Strandlengjan frá Kvíármýrarkambi og alla leið að Höfn býður upp á einstaka nálægð við Vatnajökul. Jökulmótað landslagið og fjaran skapa aðstæður fyrir fjölbreytt dýralíf, rannsóknir og ferðamennsku.

17. apríl 2026

![](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2FaeINup1ZCF7ETSWi_2026n%25C3%25A1tt%25C3%25BAra.isvefurLANDSCAPE.png%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfaces%252Cedges%26fit%3Dcrop%26w%3D1640%26h%3D820&w=3840&q=80)

Náttúruverndarstofnun auglýsir laust til umsóknar starf aðstoðarþjóðgarðsvarðar sem er einnig staðgengill þjóðgarðsvarðar á austurhluta suðursvæðis Vatnajökulsþjóðgarðs. Meginþungi starfseminnar fer fram á Breiðamerkursandi og nærliggjandi svæðum.

Aðstoðarþjóðgarðsvörður tekur virkan þátt í stjórnun og þróun þjóðgarðsins auk annarra friðlýstra svæða á suðausturlandi. Leitað er að kraftmiklum einstaklingi sem vill nýta þekkingu sína og áhuga á náttúruvernd til að taka þátt í þróun náttúruverndar, samstarfi og þjónustu við gesti á svæðinu. Um er að ræða fjölbreytt og krefjandi starf þar sem frumkvæði og sjálfstæði fá að njóta sín. Starfsstöð er á Höfn í Hornafirði.

## **Helstu verkefni og ábyrgð**

* Skipulagning landvörslu og önnur rekstrar- og mannauðsmál í umboði þjóðgarðsvarðar á svæðinu
* Ber ábyrgð á gæða- og umhverfismálum svæðisins
* Leiðir fræðslumál svæðisins
* Eftirfylgni framkvæmdaverkefna
* Leyfismál á starfssvæði
* Samskipti við hagsmunaaðila á svæðinu
* Tekur þátt í þróun og stefnumótun svæðisins

## **Hæfniskröfur**

* Háskólamenntun sem nýtist í starfi
* Mjög góð samskipta- og samstarfsfærni
* Geta til að þróa hugmyndir og verklag
* Frumkvæði og sjálfstæði í starfi
* Áhugi og þekking á náttúruvernd og umhverfismálum
* Þekking á starfsemi þjóðgarða og friðlýstra svæða er kostur
* Þekking og reynsla af landvörslu er kostur
* Gott vald á íslensku og ensku

## **Frekari upplýsingar um starfið**

Laun samkvæmt gildandi kjarasamningi sem fjármála- og efnahagsráðherra og viðkomandi stéttarfélag hafa gert.

Vinsamlegast látið ferliskrá og kynningarbréf fylgja umsókninni þar sem færð eru rök fyrir hæfni í starfið.Við hvetjum áhugasöm til að sækja um óháð kyni og uppruna.Öllum umsóknum verður svarað þegar ákvörðun um ráðningu hefur verið tekin.

Nánari upplýsingar veitir Margrét Jóna Ísólfsdóttir mannauðsstjóri og Inga Dóra Hrólfsdóttir sviðsstjóri Landvörslusviðs.

Inga Dóra Hrólfsdóttir

Tölvupóstur: [email protected]

Margrét Jóna Ísólfsdóttir

## **Sótt er um hér:**

## **Nánar um svæðið**

# [39] https://www.vatnajokulsthjodgardur.is/frettir/lausstorfsnaefellsstofa

# Laus störf: Sérfæðingur í þjónustu og miðlun og landvörður á austursvæði Vatnajökulsþjóðgarðs

Náttúruverndarstofnun leitar að metnaðarfullum og jákvæðum einstaklingum til starfa á austursvæði Vatnajökulsþjóðgarðs og í Snæfellsstofu.

7. apríl 2026

![](/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fimages.prismic.io%2Fvatnajokulsthjodgardur%2Fbe4d6121-9f77-495e-96b6-e6b21edef630_Sn%25C3%25A6fellsstofa_RK.JPG%3Fauto%3Dformat%26crop%3Dfaces%252Cedges%26fit%3Dcrop%26w%3D1640%26h%3D820&w=3840&q=80)

### **Sérfræðingur í þjónustu og miðlun**

Náttúruverndarstofnun óskar eftir að ráða jákvæðan og þjónustulundaðan einstakling til að hafa umsjón með þjónustu við gesti Vatnajökulsþjóðgarðs sem heimsækja Snæfellsstofu á Skriðuklaustri. Starfið er fjölbreytt og áhugavert og felst meðal annars í umsjón með daglegum rekstri Snæfellsstofu, fræðslu, miðlun og móttöku gesta.

Sérfræðingurinn heyrir beint undir þjóðgarðsvörð austursvæðis Vatnajökulsþjóðgarðs og hefur starfsaðstöðu í Snæfellsstofu og á Egilsstöðum. Um áhugavert heilsársstarf er að ræða.

**Nánari upplýsingar og umsókn**

Ragna Fanney Jóhannsdóttir, Þjóðgarðsvörður, tölvupóstur: [email protected]

Ýttu hér fyrir nánari upplýsingar og til að sækja um

### **Landvörður á austursvæði Vatnajökulsþjóðgarðs**

Náttúruverndarstofnun leitar að metnaðarfullum og jákvæðum einstaklingi til starfa á austursvæði Vatnajökulsþjóðgarðs og í Snæfellsstofu. Landverðir taka þátt í fjölbreyttum og mikilvægum verkefnum sem stuðla að verndun og varðveislu náttúrunnar. Verkefni landvarða fela meðal annars í sér að efla innviði og umhirðu svæða, leiðbeina og fræða gesti um náttúru landsins, fylgjast með ástandi umhverfisins. Landverðir gegna lykilhlutverki í að fræða gesti um náttúruna og hvetja til ábyrgðar í umgengni við hana.

Austursvæði Vatnajökulsþjóðgarðs nær frá Jökulsá á Fjöllum að mörkum friðlandsins Lónsöræfum. Landvarsla er bæði á hálendi í Krepputungu og Snæfelli og á láglendi í Snæfellsstofu á Skriðuklaustri.

Um ótímabundið starf er að ræða.

**Nánari upplýsingar og umsókn**

Ragna Fanney Jóhannsdóttir, Þjóðgarðsvörður, tölvupóstur: [email protected]

Ýttu hér fyrir nánari upplýsingar og til að sækja um
